        Don't show againThe error in the console logs ("StorageApiError: new row violates row-level security policy" with 400 status) is another RLS violation during the PDS PDF upload in the /phase1c stub page. This is because the path uses 'pds/user_{id}/file.pdf' for product data sheets (e.g., Tremco membrane VOC limits or substrate compatibilities), but our current RLS policies only allow [1] IN ('jobs', 'submittals')—'pds' isn't included, so the insert fails server-side despite authenticated role.
This ties to Enki's Phase 1 risk reduction: secure storage for vendor PDS to flag substrate mismatches (e.g., alkali-sensitive membranes on fresh concrete) in spec matching, protecting profit in submittals or procurement. To fix, update the RLS policies in Supabase dashboard to include 'pds' in the allowed phases. No code changes needed—the upload path is correct for organization (separating PDS from submittals/jobs).
Fix: Update RLS Policies in Supabase Dashboard
Go to Storage > Policies for 'enki-storage' bucket. Delete the existing phase/user path policies, then add these updated ones (add 'pds' to the IN list for scalability to Phase 1C DB prep).
Insert Policy:
SQL-- Name: Allow authenticated users to insert into their own phase/user path
create policy "User insert own phase/user path"
on storage.objects for insert
with check (
  bucket_id = 'enki-storage'
  and auth.role() = 'authenticated'
  and (storage.foldername(name))[1] IN ('jobs', 'submittals', 'pds')
  and (storage.foldername(name))[2] = concat('user_', auth.uid())
);
Select Policy:
SQL-- Name: Allow authenticated users to read their own phase/user path
create policy "User select own phase/user path"
on storage.objects for select
using (
  bucket_id = 'enki-storage'
  and auth.role() = 'authenticated'
  and (storage.foldername(name))[1] IN ('jobs', 'submittals', 'pds')
  and (storage.foldername(name))[2] = concat('user_', auth.uid())
);
Update Policy:
SQL-- Name: Allow authenticated users to update their own phase/user path
create policy "User update own phase/user path"
on storage.objects for update
with check (
  bucket_id = 'enki-storage'
  and auth.role() = 'authenticated'
  and (storage.foldername(name))[1] IN ('jobs', 'submittals', 'pds')
  and (storage.foldername(name))[2] = concat('user_', auth.uid())
);

PS C:\Enki-App\apps\web>Acknowledged—the terminal output shows a successful installation of Jest and its TypeScript-related dependencies (jest, @types/jest, ts-jest, jest-ts) in your Enki web app directory, adding 247 packages with no vulnerabilities. The warnings are non-critical but should be addressed for long-term maintainability—deprecated packages like inflight (from older glob dependencies) can lead to memory leaks or incompatibility in future Next.js/Supabase updates, potentially affecting PM workflows like invoice rate consistency checks.
Addressing the Warnings

inflight@1.0.6 deprecated: This is a legacy utility for coalescing async requests, pulled in by older dependencies (likely from glob or rimraf in Jest's tree). It's unsupported and leaky—recommend migrating to lru-cache (a robust alternative for caching, useful if we expand Enki to cache parsed risks or vendor PDS details in 1C).
glob@7.2.3 deprecated: This file pattern matcher is outdated (pre-v9); it's likely from ts-jest or jest-ts internals. Versions <9 are no longer supported, risking bugs in file resolution (e.g., during tests for upload-zone.tsx handling specs PDFs).

These are common in fresh Jest setups with TS—installation succeeded, but update dependencies to silence them and ensure stability for Enki's browser-based PWA (e.g., reliable testing of generateFromRisks for jurisdiction-aware VOC clauses in submittals).
Recommended Fixes

Update Deprecated Dependencies: Run npm update glob inflight to pull newer versions (glob to v10+, which drops inflight). If locked, add to package.json: "glob": "^10.0.0", then npm i.
Review jest-ts: This package is less standard (possibly a typo for ts-jest?); ts-jest is the official for TS support in Jest. If jest-ts isn't essential, remove it (npm uninstall jest-ts) and rely on ts-jest.
Run Audit/Fund: As noted, npm fund for supporting packages; npm audit fix if future vulnerabilities appear.

Once complete, we'll summarize Phase 1C scraping (modular Selenium for Tremco/BASF PDS, change detection hashes) and start a new chat.Let's proceed with Jest install warnings furtherBased on the Jest installation warnings you encountered (deprecated inflight@1.0.6 and glob@7.2.3), these are common in TypeScript/Jest setups due to transitive dependencies (e.g., rimraf or fs-extra pulling in outdated glob, which in turn uses inflight for async caching). While not blocking for Enki's Phase 1 (they don't affect runtime for PM workflows like upload/parse for substrate risks in spec matching or lead time flagging in procurement), they can lead to memory leaks or incompatibility in tests/debugging. Ignoring them risks future bugs in deployment (e.g., standalone build for Vercel in office/trailer use).
Causes

inflight@1.0.6: Obsolete async coalescer with known memory leaks (replaced by lru-cache in modern deps).
glob@7.2.3: Old file pattern matcher; v9+ is supported, but older Jest/ts-jest chains pull it in.

From web searches, this is a known Jest issue (#15926, #15087 on GitHub), often resolved by forcing newer versions via npm resolutions or updates.
Fix Steps

Edit package.json: Add a "resolutions" section to force updates (this overrides deps in the tree). Also update glob directly if listed.Open package.json in your web app root (C:\Enki-App\apps\web) and add/update:JSON{
  // ... existing content
  "dependencies": {
    // ... 
    "glob": "^10.0.0" // Force latest glob if not present; add this line
  },
  "resolutions": {
    "glob": "^10.0.0", // Override any older glob in deps
    "inflight": "npm:lru-cache@^10.0.0" // Replace inflight with lru-cache alias
  }
}
Run Updates: In terminal at C:\Enki-App\apps\web:textnpm install
npm audit fix --forceThis updates the lockfile and resolves deprecations. If errors, run npm dedupe after.
Verify: Re-run npm test or npm i—warnings should disappear. Check node_modules/glob version (should be 10+).

This ensures stable testing for Enki's refinements (e.g., Jest for ai-generate.ts clauses with CA VOC context). If warnings persist, share updated terminal output for further tweaks (e.g., specific dep pinning). test('parseFiles returns focus-specific risks', () => {
  const mockFiles: File[] = [];
  const risks = parseFiles(mockFiles, { focus: 'procurement' });
  expect(risks).toContain('Risk: Lead time for flashing materials exceeds GC schedule');
});
tests/ai-generate.test.ts (Verifies context-aware outputs for clauses, e.g., VOC per jurisdiction in submittals):TypeScriptimport { generateFromRisks } from '@/lib/ai-generate';

test('generateFromRisks returns context-aware clauses', async () => {
  const mockRisks = ['Risk: VOC compliance violation'];
  const clauses = await generateFromRisks(mockRisks, { type: 'clauses', context: { jurisdiction: 'CA' } });
  expect(clauses[0]).toContain('(per CA regs)');
});

Now, as per your instruction, here's a detailed summary of what we discussed regarding Phase 1C (Manufacturer Product Database for spec matching and substrate risk flagging) from the initial chat focus, before starting a new chat for it.
Summary of Phase 1C Discussions
We focused on gathering product data sheets (PDS) for waterproofing vendors (Tremco, BASF, Carlisle, Sika, Henry, expanding to Soprema/GCP/Polyglass/W.R. Meadows) to populate the DB for spec matching (e.g., flagging incompatible materials on concrete or VOC limits per jurisdiction to protect profit in submittals/procurement). Key ideas bounced:

Vendor Prioritization: Start with top 5 globals (Tremco/Sika for sealants/membranes, Carlisle for roofing-integrated); categorize by product (fluid-applied/sheet goods/below-grade); include regionals like CETCO for bentonite if US-focused. Use CSV seed list for script config.
Scraping Strategy: Hybrid requests/BeautifulSoup for static pages, Selenium for dynamic/JS-heavy sites (e.g., tremcosealants.com/products grid with filters/modals). Handle challenges: Anti-scraping (delays 2-5s, user-agent rotation, proxies); Cookies (Selenium session persistence); PDF extraction (click downloads, monitor folder via Chrome prefs); Relevance (filter by "technical data sheet" or keywords like "VOC limits", "lead times", "substrate compatibility").
Modularity and Change Detection: Vendor-specific modules (e.g., def scrape_tremco(url): ... in main script); Config YAML/JSON for URLs/categories. For site changes: Pre-scrape HTML hash (MD5 of DOM/key elements like product links); Compare to baseline in Supabase/config; Alert on mismatch (email/Slack/webhook) and fallback to generic crawler (keyword search); Smoke tests (assert >5 PDS links found).
Output for Enki Integration: Structured folders (/data/Tremco/Vulkem_116/PDS.pdf); Metadata CSV (product_name, pdf_path, scrape_date) for upload to Supabase table (for 1C DB); Optional PDF parse (PyPDF2 for quick VOC/warranty extraction) to prep AI matching.
Tech Stack/Ethics: Python 3.8+ with selenium/webdriver-manager (headless Chrome); BeautifulSoup/requests fallback; Multithreading for parallel vendors (cautious with resources). Respectful scraping (delays, robots.txt compliance, no overload); Docker for reproducibility.
Waterproofing Focus: Filter for "below-grade" or "plaza deck" keywords; Embed jurisdiction/material params in outputs for Enki's AI (e.g., flag "Lead time: 4-6 weeks" for procurement discipline or "Not compatible with alkali" for substrate risks in job setup).

This modular approach (e.g., per-vendor functions with hash monitoring) ensures sustainable PDS gathering for 1C's spec matching, reducing risks like delayed submittals from outdated data.

Ran all test suites.The Jest test failures you're seeing are a standard setup issue in Next.js/TypeScript projects: Jest isn't configured to transpile JSX (React syntax like <UploadZone />) or TypeScript features (e.g., type annotations like const mockFiles: File[] = []), leading to parse errors. This doesn't affect Enki's runtime (e.g., PM workflows like drag-drop uploads for specs parsing in job setup or risk flagging for substrate incompatibilities in submittals), but it's critical for Step 8's testing (ensuring ai-parse.ts mocks like "Risk: Lead time for flashing materials exceeds GC schedule" work for procurement discipline).
The "experimental syntax 'jsx'" error requires adding Babel presets for React; the TS initializer errors need ts-jest to handle types. We'll fix this by updating jest.config.ts (add transform for JSX/TS), adding babel.config.js (for React preset), and ensuring test files are in a tests folder (Jest default—your output shows C:\Enki-App\apps\web_tests_... , so that's good).
Fix Steps

Update jest.config.ts (at web app root): Add Babel transform for JSX, ensure ts-jest for TS.
Copy-paste this full code to replace your existing jest.config.ts.TypeScriptimport type { Config } from 'jest';

const config: Config = {
  verbose: true,
  testEnvironment: 'jsdom', // Changed to jsdom for React/JSX support (browser-like env)
  transform: {
    '^.+\\.(ts|tsx)$': 'ts-jest', // TS/TSX with ts-jest
    '^.+\\.(js|jsx)$': 'babel-jest' // JS/JSX with Babel for React syntax
  },
  moduleNameMapper: {
    '^@/(.*)$': '<rootDir>/$1', // For @/* paths
  },
  setupFilesAfterEnv: ['<rootDir>/jest.setup.ts'], // Optional: Create if needed for global setup
  globals: {
    'ts-jest': {
      tsconfig: 'tsconfig.json' // Point to your tsconfig for type checking
    }
  }
};

export default config;
Add babel.config.js (create at web app root): For React preset to handle JSX.
Copy-paste this full code into the new file.JavaScriptmodule.exports = {
  presets: [
    '@babel/preset-env', // Modern JS
    '@babel/preset-react', // JSX/React
    '@babel/preset-typescript' // TS support
  ]
};
Install Babel Deps: Run in terminal at C:\Enki-App\apps\web:textnpm i -D babel-jest @babel/preset-env @babel/preset-react @babel/preset-typescript

Copy-paste the following full code into jest.config.ts (use VS Code or your editor for proper TS formatting).
TypeScriptimport type { Config } from 'jest';

const config: Config = {
  verbose: true,
  testEnvironment: 'jsdom', // For React/JSX support (browser-like env)
  transform: {
    '^.+\\.(ts|tsx)$': 'ts-jest', // TS/TSX with ts-jest
    '^.+\\.(js|jsx)$': 'babel-jest' // JS/JSX with Babel for React syntax
  },
  moduleNameMapper: {
    '^@/(.*)$': '<rootDir>/$1', // For @/* paths in imports
  },
  setupFilesAfterEnv: ['<rootDir>/jest.setup.ts'], // Optional: Create if needed for globals
  globals: {
    'ts-jest': {
      tsconfig: 'tsconfig.json' // Point to your tsconfig for type checking
    }
  }
};

export default config;

Addressing the Warning
whatwg-encoding is deprecated in favor of @exodus/bytes for better spec compliance and speed (e.g., faster handling of binary data in PDS PDF extracts for lead times or substrate compatibilities). It's a transitive dep from jest-environment-jsdom/html-encoding-sniffer, so we'll force an override via resolutions in package.json to use the recommended alternative, maintaining Enki's efficiency for risk reduction (e.g., quick flags for unpriced extras in change orders).
Update your package.json with this resolutions block (add if not present, or merge):
JSON"resolutions": {
  "whatwg-encoding": "npm:@exodus/bytes@^1.0.0" // Override to recommended replacement for faster/spec-conformant encoding
}
Then run npm install to apply. This should silence the warning without breaking jsdom (test with npm test—expect no deprecations).

Summary of Phase 1C Discussions
As per your instruction, here's the detailed summary from our earlier bounce on Phase 1C (Manufacturer Product Database for spec matching and substrate risk flagging, focusing on waterproofing vendors like Tremco/BASF/Carlisle/Sika/Henry for PDS with VOC limits, lead times, compatibilities, costs, warranties).
We discussed a modular, automated scraping system using Python/Selenium for dynamic sites (e.g., tremcosealants.com products with JS grids/filters/modals for downloading PDS PDFs). Key ideas:

Vendor Selection/Prioritization: Start with top 5 (Tremco/Sika for sealants/membranes, Carlisle for roofing-integrated, BASF/Henry for below-grade); expand to Soprema/GCP/Polyglass/W.R. Meadows or regionals like CETCO for bentonite. Categorize by product (fluid-applied/sheet goods/plaza deck); use CSV/YAML config for URLs/categories to add new vendors easily.
Scraping Strategy: Hybrid requests/BeautifulSoup for static fetches (faster), Selenium for JS-heavy (headless Chrome with webdriver-manager for auto-driver, simulating clicks on "Download PDS"); Handle anti-scraping (random 2-5s delays, user-agent rotation, proxies if rate-limited); Cookies (Selenium session capture/persist); PDF extraction (monitor download folder via Chrome prefs, filter by title/URL like "technical data sheet" or keywords "VOC limits", "lead times", "substrate compatibility"); Ignore irrelevant (e.g., MSDS/install guides).
Modularity/Change Detection: Vendor-specific functions (e.g., def scrape_tremco(url): ...); Main script with config for parallel runs (multithreading cautious for resources). For site changes: Pre-scrape HTML hash (MD5 of DOM/key elements like product links); Compare to stored baseline (Supabase table/config file); If mismatch, alert (email/Slack/webhook to Enki devs) and fallback to generic crawler (keyword search); Automated smoke tests (assert >5 PDS links found pre-scrape).
Output/Integration: Structured folders (/data/Tremco/Vulkem_116/PDS.pdf); Metadata CSV (product_name, pdf_path, scrape_date, extracted_snippets like VOC values) for upload to Supabase table (for 1C DB queries in spec matching); Optional post-scrape PDF parse (PyPDF2 for quick wins on warranties/costs); ZIP archives for drag-drop into Enki PWA.
Tech/Ethics/Reliability: Python 3.8+ with selenium, beautifulsoup4, requests, PyPDF2; Virtualenv/Docker for reproducibility; Cron-friendly for weekly refreshes (to keep lead times current for procurement). Ethical: Respect robots.txt, no overload (limits to 10-20 PDFs/run), assume public PDS access; Unit tests for scrapers (mock pages with BeautifulSoup).

This approach ensures reliable PDS gathering for 1C's AI-assisted matching (flagging risks like "Adhesive not compatible with alkali-prone concrete" to reduce rework in change orders).

  https://jestjs.io/docs/configurationThe validation error in your npm test output is due to jest.config.ts referencing a non-existent jest.setup.ts file in the setupFilesAfterEnv option. This option is for global test setup (e.g., extending expect matchers for React Testing Library in UploadZone tests), but since our Enki Phase 1 tests are simple stubs (e.g., verifying ai-parse.ts mocks for lead time risks in procurement or substrate incompatibilities in submittals), it's optional and can be removed to avoid the error. This doesn't affect runtime stability for PM workflows (e.g., secure RLS uploads for invoice rate consistency checks or warranty flagging in closeout), but ensures tests run cleanly for profit-protecting patterns like generateFromRisks clauses with jurisdiction context for VOC compliance.
Fix Steps

Update jest.config.ts: Remove the setupFilesAfterEnv line (or create the file if you want global setup—see optional below). Copy-paste this full updated code to replace your jest.config.ts at the web app root.TypeScriptimport type { Config } from 'jest';

const config: Config = {
  verbose: true,
  testEnvironment: 'jsdom', // For React/JSX support (browser-like env)
  transform: {
    '^.+\\.(ts|tsx)$': 'ts-jest', // TS/TSX with ts-jest
    '^.+\\.(js|jsx)$': 'babel-jest' // JS/JSX with Babel for React syntax
  },
  moduleNameMapper: {
    '^@/(.*)$': '<rootDir>/$1', // For @/* paths in imports
  },
  globals: {
    'ts-jest': {
      tsconfig: 'tsconfig.json' // Point to your tsconfig for type checking
    }
  }
};

export default config;
Optional: Create jest.setup.ts (if you want to add globals later, e.g., for more React tests). Create at root and add this code—then keep the setupFilesAfterEnv line in jest.config.ts.TypeScriptimport '@testing-library/jest-dom'; // Extends expect with matchers like toBeInTheDocument for UploadZone tests

Analysis of Issues

ts-jest Depreciation Warning (Repeated 3 Times): The config uses the old "globals" format for ts-jest; v29+ recommends embedding in the "transform" object for better modularity. This warns but doesn't fail tests.
Failure in upload-zone.test.tsx: Missing module '@testing-library/react' (needed for render/screen in React component tests like UploadZone's drag-drop for specs PDFs). This blocks the suite.
Overall: 1 suite failed due to import, but the other 2 passed—good sign for AI stubs. No snapshots/time issues.

Fixes

Update jest.config.ts: Switch ts-jest to transform format to silence deprecations. Copy-paste this full updated code to replace your jest.config.ts at the web app root.TypeScriptimport type { Config } from 'jest';

const config: Config = {
  verbose: true,
  testEnvironment: 'jsdom',
  transform: {
    '^.+\\.(ts|tsx)$': ['ts-jest', { tsconfig: 'tsconfig.json' }], // Updated: Embed ts-jest config here
    '^.+\\.(js|jsx)$': 'babel-jest'
  },
  moduleNameMapper: {
    '^@/(.*)$': '<rootDir>/$1',
  },
  setupFilesAfterEnv: ['<rootDir>/jest.setup.ts'] // Keep if you have it; optional
};

export default config;
Install Missing Deps: Run in terminal at C:\Enki-App\apps\web:textnpm i -D @testing-library/react @testing-library/jest-dom

Summary of Phase 1C Discussions
As per your instruction, here's the detailed summary of our earlier discussions on Phase 1C (Manufacturer Product Database for spec matching and substrate risk flagging, focusing on waterproofing vendors like Tremco/BASF/Carlisle/Sika/Henry for PDS with VOC limits, lead times, compatibilities, costs, warranties).
We discussed a modular, automated scraping system using Python/Selenium for dynamic sites (e.g., tremcosealants.com products with JS grids/filters/modals for downloading PDS PDFs). Key ideas bounced:

Vendor Selection/Prioritization: Start with top 5 globals (Tremco/Sika for sealants/membranes, Carlisle for roofing-integrated); expand to Soprema/GCP/Polyglass/W.R. Meadows or regionals like CETCO for bentonite. Categorize by product (fluid-applied/sheet goods/plaza deck); use CSV/YAML config for URLs/categories to add new vendors easily.
Scraping Strategy: Hybrid requests/BeautifulSoup for static fetches (faster), Selenium for JS-heavy (headless Chrome with webdriver-manager for auto-driver, simulating clicks on "Download PDS"); Handle anti-scraping (random 2-5s delays, user-agent rotation, proxies if rate-limited); Cookies (Selenium session capture/persist); PDF extraction (monitor download folder via Chrome prefs, filter by title/URL like "technical data sheet" or keywords "VOC limits", "lead times", "substrate compatibility"); Ignore irrelevant (e.g., MSDS/install guides).
Modularity/Change Detection: Vendor-specific functions (e.g., def scrape_tremco(url): ...); Main script with config for parallel runs (multithreading cautious for resources). For site changes: Pre-scrape HTML hash (MD5 of DOM/key elements like product links); Compare to stored baseline (Supabase table/config file); If mismatch, alert (email/Slack/webhook to Enki devs) and fallback to generic crawler (keyword search); Automated smoke tests (assert >5 PDS links found pre-scrape).
Output/Integration: Structured folders (/data/Tremco/Vulkem_116/PDS.pdf); Metadata CSV (product_name, pdf_path, scrape_date, extracted_snippets like VOC values) for upload to Supabase table (for 1C DB queries in spec matching); Optional post-scrape PDF parse (PyPDF2 for quick wins on warranties/costs); ZIP archives for drag-drop into Enki PWA.
Tech/Ethics/Reliability: Python 3.8+ with selenium, beautifulsoup4, requests, PyPDF2; Virtualenv/Docker for reproducibility; Cron-friendly for weekly refreshes (to keep lead times current for procurement). Ethical: Respect robots.txt, no overload (limits to 10-20 PDFs/run), assume public PDS access; Unit tests for scrapers (mock pages with BeautifulSoup).

This approach ensures reliable PDS gathering for 1C's AI-assisted matching (flagging risks like "Adhesive not compatible with alkali-prone concrete" to reduce rework in change orders).
